\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-1}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{8})\,{\rm{d}}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\|Aw(s)\|^{2}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{8}\right){\rm{d}}\tau\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-1}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{8})\,{\rm{d}}\eta}\Big(\|Aw(s)\|^{2}+1\Big),

where in the last inequality we have used the basic relation
x\leq e^{x}
for
x\geq 0
.
Integrating w.r.t.
s
over
[t-1,t]
, we can get that

\displaystyle\|Aw(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-1}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\eta)\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{8})\,{\rm{d}}\eta}\left(\int_{t-1}^{t}\|Aw(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s+1\right).

Then we replace
\omega
with
\theta_{-t}\omega
to deduce

\displaystyle\|Aw(t,\theta_{-t}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\eta,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{8}{\rm{d}}\eta+C\int^{0}_{-1}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{8}\,{\rm{d}}\eta}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\int_{t-1}^{t}\|Aw(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s+1\right).

(4.22)

By (
4.12
), it can get that

\displaystyle\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{8}\,{\rm{d}}s\leq|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{4},\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega).

(4.23)

Moreover, since
u
is a trajectory within the global attractor
\mathcal{A}_{0}
which is bounded in
H^{2}
, by (
4.12
) again we can deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{t-1}^{t}\|Aw(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s+2\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A\textbf{u}\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s+2\|\mathcal{A}\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{\nu}\,\zeta_{3}(\omega)+C,\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega).

(4.24)

Hence, inserting (
4.23
) and (
4.24
) into (
4.1
) we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|Aw(t,\theta_{-t}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{4}+C\int^{0}_{-1}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{8}\,{\rm{d}}\eta}\left(\zeta_{3}(\omega)+1\right)

\displaystyle=:\zeta_{5}(\omega),\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega).

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

4.2
H^{2}
random absorbing sets

By Lemma
4.2
we can construct an
H^{2}
random absorbing set of the random anisotropic (NS) equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise. Nevertheless, for later purpose we make the following stronger estimate than Lemma
4.2
.
It will be crucial in the next section in deriving the local
(H,H^{2})
-Lipschitz continuity of the equations.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. There exists a tempered random variable
\rho(\cdot)
such that the solutions
v
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise and the solutions
u
of the deterministic anisotropic NS equations (
3.5
) satisfy

\sup_{\varepsilon\in[0,1]}\|A\textbf{v}(t,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))-A\textbf{u}(t,\textbf{u}_{0})\|^{2}\leq\rho(\omega),\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega),

whenever
\textbf{v}(0)\in\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega)
and
\textbf{u}(0)\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, where
T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\cdot)
is given in (
4.11
).

Proof.

By (
4.21
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|Aw\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)\|Aw\|^{2}+C\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{8}\right)\left(\|Aw\|^{2}+1\right).

(4.25)

For any
t\geq 1
, integrating the inequality (
4.2
) from
(s,t)
for
s\in(t-1,t)
and then integrating over
s\in(t-1,t)
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|Aw(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t-1}^{t}\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s)\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{8}\right)\left(\|Aw(s)\|^{2}+1\right){\rm{d}}s.

For any
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
, replacing
\omega
with
\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega
yields

\displaystyle\|Aw(t,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{t-1}^{t}\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s-t-\varepsilon}\omega)|^{8}\right)\left(\|Aw(s)\|^{2}+1\right){\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{t+\varepsilon-2}^{t+\varepsilon}\left(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{8}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s-t-\varepsilon}\omega)|^{8}\right)\left(\|Aw(s)\|^{2}+1\right){\rm{d}}s.

Note that from (
4.12
) it can deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(t-2,t)}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\eta,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{8}

\displaystyle\leq|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{4},\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega).

Hence, let

\displaystyle\zeta_{6}(\omega):=|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{4}+\sup_{s\in(-2,0)}|z(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{8}+1

we given a tempered random variable
\zeta_{6}
such that

\displaystyle\|Aw(t,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}\leq C\zeta_{6}(\omega)\int_{t+\varepsilon-2}^{t+\varepsilon}\!\big(\|Aw(s,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}+1\big)\,{\rm{d}}s

for any
t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)
uniformly for
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
.
By (
4.24
), we can have that

\displaystyle\int_{t+\varepsilon-2}^{t+\varepsilon}\|Aw(s,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s\leq\frac{4}{\nu}\,\zeta_{3}(\omega)+C

for any
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)
, hence we can deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in[0,1]}\|Aw(t,\theta_{-t-\varepsilon}\omega,w(0))\|^{2}\leq C\zeta_{6}(\omega)\left(\zeta_{3}(\omega)+1\right)=:\rho(\omega)

(4.26)

for any
t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)
. This completes the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

Let
\varepsilon=0
, then we can construct an
H^{2}
random absorbing set.

Theorem 4.4

(
H^{2}
absorbing set)
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. The RDS
\phi
generated by the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise has a random absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}_{H^{2}}
, defined as a random
H^{2}
neighborhood of the global attractor
\mathcal{A}_{0}
of the deterministic anisotropic NS equations:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{H^{2}}(\omega)=\left\{\textbf{v}\in H^{2}:\,{\rm dist}_{H^{2}}(\textbf{v},\mathcal{A}_{0})\leq\sqrt{\rho(\omega)}\,\right\},\quad\omega\in\Omega,

where
\rho(\cdot)
is the tempered random variable defined by (
4.26
). As a consequence, the
\mathcal{D}_{H}
-random attractor
\mathcal{A}
of (
3.17
) is a bounded and tempered random set in
H^{2}
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
4.3
, see also
[
21
, Theorem 13]
.∎

5
The (
H,H^{2}
)-smoothing effect

In this section, we will prove the (
H,H^{2}
)-smoothing effect of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise. This effect is essentially a
local
(H,H^{2})
-Lipschitz continuity in initial values. It will be shown step-by-step by proving the local Lipschitz continuity in
H
, the
(H,H^{1})
-smoothing and finally the
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing. For simplicity, we set

\bar{v}(t,\omega,\bar{v}(0)):=\textbf{v}_{1}(t,\omega,\textbf{v}_{1}(0))-\textbf{v}_{2}(t,\omega,\textbf{v}_{2}(0))

the difference between two solutions.

5.1
Lipschitz continuity in
H

Lemma 5.1
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
.
For any tempered set
\mathfrak{D}\in\mathcal{D}_{H}
, then there is random variables
t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\cdot)
and
L_{1}(\mathfrak{D},\cdot)
such that any
two solutions
\textbf{v}_{1}
and
\textbf{v}_{2}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}
in
\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega),
respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\textbf{v}_{1}\!\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0}\right)-\textbf{v}_{2}\!\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\textbf{v}_{2,0}\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{1}({\mathfrak{D}},\omega)\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}-\textbf{v}_{2,0}\|^{2},\quad\omega\in\Omega.

Proof.

The difference
\bar{v}=\textbf{v}_{1}-\textbf{v}_{2}
of solutions of system (
3.17
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}\bar{v}}{{\rm{d}}t}+\nu A_{1}\bar{v}+B(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))-B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))=0.

(5.1)

Taking the inner product of the system (
5.1
) with
\bar{v}
in
H
, by integration by parts, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|\bar{v}\|^{2}+\nu\|\partial_{y}\bar{v}_{1}\|^{2}+\nu\|\partial_{x}\bar{v}_{2}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),\bar{v}\big)-\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),\bar{v}\big)

\displaystyle=-\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),\bar{v}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{v}\|^{2}_{L^{4}}\|\nabla(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bar{v}\|\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{4}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right)\|\bar{v}\|^{2}.

By direct computations and (
3.16
), it yields

\displaystyle\frac{\nu}{2}\|\bar{v}\|^{2}\leq\nu\|\partial_{y}\bar{v}_{1}\|^{2}+\nu\|\partial_{x}\bar{v}_{2}\|^{2}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|\bar{v}\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\leq C\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right)\|\bar{v}\|^{2}.

By using Gronwall’s lemma, we can get that

\displaystyle\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2},\quad t>0.

(5.2)

By virtue of (
3.26
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq e^{\left(\frac{4}{\alpha}-3\right)\lambda t}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\left(\frac{4}{\alpha}-3\right)\lambda(\tau-t)}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\alpha\nu}e^{\left(\frac{4}{\alpha}-3\right)\lambda t}\left(e^{-\lambda t}\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}\|^{2}+\zeta_{1}(\omega)\right),\quad t\geq T_{1}(\omega),

so for (
5.1
) we also can get

\displaystyle\|\bar{v}(t,\theta_{-t}\omega,\bar{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}C\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau-t}\omega)|^{2}\right){\rm{d}}\tau}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{Ce^{\left(\frac{4}{\alpha}-3\right)\lambda t}\left(e^{-\lambda t}\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}\|^{2}+\zeta_{1}(\omega)\right)+C\int_{-t}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}

for any
t\geq T_{1}(\omega)
.
Since
\textbf{v}_{1,0}
belongs to
\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-t}\omega)
which is tempered, there exists a random variable
t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)\geq T_{1}(\omega)
such that

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}\|^{2}\leq e^{-\lambda t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\left\|\,\mathfrak{D}\!\left(\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega\right)\right\|^{2}\leq 1,\quad\omega\in\Omega.

Moreover, since
\mathfrak{D}
is pullback absorbed by the absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
, this exists a large enough
t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)
such that

\displaystyle\phi\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega)\right)\subset\mathfrak{B}(\omega),\quad\omega\in\Omega.

(5.3)

Therefore, a random variable given by as follows

\displaystyle L_{1}({\mathfrak{D}},\omega)=e^{Ce^{\left(\frac{4}{\alpha}-3\right)\lambda t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}(1+\zeta_{1}(\omega))+C\int_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\tau},\quad\omega\in\Omega,

then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{v}\!\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\bar{v}(0)\right)\right\|^{2}\leq L_{1}({\mathfrak{D}},\omega)\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2},

(5.4)

whenever
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}\in\mathfrak{D}\big(\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega\big),

\omega\in\Omega
. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

5.2
(H,H^{1})
-smoothing

In this subsection, the
(H,H^{1})
-smoothing will be shown by two steps. Since we have constructed by (
3.35
) an
H^{1}
random absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
, we begin with initial values lying in the absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
, and then consider the initial values in every tempered set
\mathfrak{D}
in
\mathcal{D}_{H}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

[
(H,H^{1})
-smoothing on
\mathfrak{B}
]
Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. For the random absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
given by (
3.35
) there are random variables
\tau_{\omega}
and
L_{2}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)
such that any
two solutions
\textbf{v}_{1}
and
\textbf{v}_{2}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}
in
\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),
respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})-\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{2,0})\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq L_{2}({\mathfrak{B}},\omega)\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}-\textbf{v}_{2,0}\|^{2},\quad\omega\in\Omega.

(5.5)

Proof.

Taking the inner product of the system (
5.1
) with
A\bar{v}
in
H
, by integration by parts, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\bar{v}_{1}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}{\rm{d}}x+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\bar{v}_{2}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}{\rm{d}}x

\displaystyle=-\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),A\bar{v}\big)-\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),A\bar{v}\big).

(5.6)

By direct computations and similar method, we also have

\displaystyle\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\bar{v}_{1}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}{\rm{d}}x+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\bar{v}_{2}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}{\rm{d}}x

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{xx}\bar{v}_{1}+\partial_{yy}\bar{v}_{1})^{2}{\rm{d}}x+\frac{1}{2}\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{xx}\bar{v}_{2}+\partial_{yy}\bar{v}_{2})^{2}{\rm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nu\|\Delta\bar{v}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\nu\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}.

(5.7)

By using the Hölder inequality, the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality and the Young inequality, we can get that

\displaystyle\big|\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),A\bar{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2},

(5.8)

and

\displaystyle\big|\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),A\bar{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|_{L^{4}}\|\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)\|_{L^{4}}\|A\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}.

(5.9)

Inserting (
5.2
)-(
5.2
) into (
5.2
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\leq C\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}.

(5.10)

By using Gronwall’s lemma, we can get that for any
s\in(t-1,t-\frac{1}{2})
,
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{s}^{t}e^{\int_{\eta}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(\eta)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(s)\|^{2},

and then integrating w.r.t.
s
over
(t-1,t-\frac{1}{2})
yields

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}e^{\int_{\eta}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(\eta)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\int_{t-1}^{t-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2e^{\int_{t-1}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\int_{t-1}^{t-\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s.

Applying (
5.1
), it yields

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}e^{\int_{\eta}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(\eta)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2e^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}+1)\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2},\quad t\geq 1.

(5.11)

Since the absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
itself belongs to the attraction universe
\mathcal{D}_{H}
, it pullback absorbs itself. Then there exists a random variable
\tau_{\omega}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\phi\big(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)\big)\subset\mathfrak{B}(\omega),\quad\omega\in\Omega.

(5.12)

We replace
\omega
with
\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega
in (
5.11
) and deduce the estimate at
t=\tau_{\omega}
that

\displaystyle\big\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\bar{v}(0))\big\|{{}^{2}}\leq 2e^{C\int_{0}^{\tau_{\omega}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}(s,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,v_{i}(0))\|^{4}{\rm{d}}s+C\int^{0}_{-\tau_{\omega}}(|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{4}+1){\rm{d}}s}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}.

(5.13)

In order to prove the bound on the right hand side of (
5.13
), by (
3.4
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right)\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{6}\right).

Since the absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
is bounded and tempered in
H^{1}
, for initial values
\textbf{v}(0)
in
\mathfrak{B}(\omega)
, applying Gronwall’s lemma, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{0}^{s}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(0)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{s}e^{\int_{\eta}^{s}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{0}^{s}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\left[\zeta_{2}(\omega)+C\int_{0}^{s}\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}\eta\right]

for any
s\geq 0
uniformly for
\textbf{v}(0)\in\mathfrak{B}(\omega)
, where
\zeta_{2}(\omega)
is the square of the radius of
\mathfrak{B}(\omega)
in
H^{1}
. As a consequence, for any
s\in(0,\tau_{\omega})
we can deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in(0,\tau_{\omega})}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{0}^{\tau_{\omega}}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\left[\zeta_{2}(\omega)+C\int_{0}^{\tau_{\omega}}\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}\eta\right],

and then, we replace
\omega
with
\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega
to get,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in(0,\tau_{\omega})}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{\int^{0}_{-\tau_{\omega}}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\left[\zeta_{2}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)+C\int^{0}_{-\tau_{\omega}}\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}\eta\right]=:\zeta_{7}(\omega).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau_{\omega}}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{4}\,{\rm{d}}s\leq\tau_{\omega}|\zeta_{7}(\omega)|^{2}

(5.14)

uniformly for any
\textbf{v}(0)
in
\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)
and thus for (
5.13
) we can get that

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\bar{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{C\tau_{\omega}|\zeta_{7}(\omega)|^{2}+C\int^{0}_{-\tau_{\omega}}(|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{4}+1){\rm{d}}s}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{C\tau_{\omega}|\zeta_{7}(\omega)|^{2}}\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}.

Hence, the random variable defined by

\displaystyle L_{2}(\mathfrak{B},\omega):=2e^{C\tau_{\omega}|\zeta_{7}(\omega)|^{2}},\quad\omega\in\Omega.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
∎

Nextly, we will prove the
(H,H^{1})
-smoothing for initial values from any tempered set
\mathfrak{D}
in
H
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. For any tempered set
\mathfrak{D}\in\mathcal{D}_{H}
there exist random variables
T_{1}(\mathfrak{D},\cdot)
and
L_{3}(\mathfrak{D},\cdot)
such that any
two solutions
\textbf{v}_{1}
and
\textbf{v}_{2}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}
in
\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-T_{1}(\mathfrak{D},\omega)}\omega),
respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\textbf{v}_{1}(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})-\textbf{v}_{2}(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{2,0})\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq L_{3}({\mathfrak{D}},\omega)\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}-\textbf{v}_{2,0}\|^{2},

(5.15)

where
T_{1}=T_{1}(\mathfrak{D},\omega)
,
\omega\in\Omega
. In addition,
v(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega,v_{0})\in\mathfrak{B}(\omega)
for any
v_{0}\in\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega)
.

Proof.

By virtue of
{\mathfrak{D}}\in\mathcal{D}_{H}
is pullback absorbed by
\mathfrak{B}
and (
5.3
), then there exists a random variable
t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\cdot)
such that

\displaystyle\phi\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega)\right)\subset\mathfrak{B}(\omega),\quad\omega\in\Omega.

(5.16)

Hence, for any
\omega\in\Omega
we can deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{v}\!\left(\tau_{\omega}+t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),\,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)}\omega,\,\bar{v}(0)\right)\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\left\|\bar{v}\!\left(\tau_{\omega},\,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\,\bar{v}\big(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),\,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)}\omega,\,\bar{v}(0)\big)\right)\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{2}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)\left\|\bar{v}\!\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),\,\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)}\omega,\,\bar{v}(0)\right)\right\|^{2}\quad\text{(by \eqref{mar18.9})}

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{2}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)L_{1}\big(\mathfrak{D},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega\big)\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}\quad\text{(by \eqref{mar18.8})}

for any
\textbf{v}_{1,0}
,
\textbf{v}_{2,0}\in\mathfrak{D}\left(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)}\omega\right)
. Let

\displaystyle T_{1}({\mathfrak{D},\omega}):=\tau_{\omega}+t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),

\displaystyle L_{3}(\mathfrak{D},\omega):=L_{2}\left(\mathfrak{B},\omega\right)L_{1}\left(\mathfrak{D},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega\right),

This completes the proof of (
5.15
).

By the definition (
5.16
) of
t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)
, we can deduce that, for any
\textbf{v}_{0}\in\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega)
,

\displaystyle y:=\textbf{v}\left(t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),\,\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)}\circ\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,\,\textbf{v}(0)\right)\in\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega).

Moreover, from the definition (
5.12
) of
\tau_{\omega}
we can deduce that

\displaystyle\textbf{ v}(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,y)\in\mathfrak{B}(\omega).

Hence, we also have

\displaystyle\textbf{v}\left(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\textbf{v}\left(\tau_{\omega}+t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),\,\theta_{-t_{\mathfrak{D}}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega)}(\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega),\,\textbf{v}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\textbf{v}(\tau_{\omega},\theta_{-\tau_{\omega}}\omega,y)\in\mathfrak{B}(\omega)

for any
v_{0}\in\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega)
, as desired. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

5.3
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing

In this subsection, we will prove the
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing property. For ease of analysis we restrict again ourselves on the absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
first. We first introduce the following main estimate.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. We say that
the solutions
v
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}(0)
satisfy the following estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[t-1,t]}\|A\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\leq 2\rho(\omega)+2\|\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2},\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)+1,

(5.17)

whenever
\textbf{v}(0)\in\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-t}\omega)
, where
T_{\mathfrak{B}}
is the random variable defined in (
4.11
).

Proof.

For any
t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)+1
and
s\in(t-1,t)
so that
s\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)
, by
Lemma
4.3
we can get that

\displaystyle\|A\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}=\|A\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-s-(t-s)}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\leq 2\rho(\omega)+2\|\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}

uniformly for
s\in[t-1,t]
and
v(0)\in\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-t}\omega)
.
∎

Lemma 5.5

(
(H^{1},H^{2})
-smoothing on
\mathfrak{B}
)
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. There exist random variables
T_{\omega}
and
L_{4}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)
such that any two solutions
\textbf{v}_{1}
and
\textbf{v}_{2}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}
in
\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega)
, respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\big\|\textbf{v}_{1}(T_{\omega},\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})-\textbf{v}_{2}(T_{\omega},\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{2,0})\big\|{{}^{2}_{H^{2}}}\leq L_{4}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}-\textbf{v}_{2,0}\|^{2}_{H^{1}},\quad\omega\in\Omega.

Proof.

Taking the inner product of the system (
5.1
) with
A^{2}\bar{v}
in
H
, by integration by parts, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}dx-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),\,A^{2}\bar{v}\big)-\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),\,A^{2}\bar{v}\big).

(5.18)

By direct computations and
\nabla\cdot\bar{v}=0
and (
3.16
), we also have

\displaystyle-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}dx-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}dx

\displaystyle=\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{y}\Delta\bar{v}_{1})^{2}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{x}\Delta\bar{v}_{2})^{2}dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\nu\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}.

(5.19)

By using the Hölder inequality, the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality and the Young inequality, we can get that

\displaystyle\big|\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),\,A^{2}\bar{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\bar{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|A(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|\big(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|+\|Ah\||y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right),

(5.20)

and

\displaystyle\big|\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),\,A^{2}\bar{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|\nabla\bar{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)\|_{L^{4}}\|A\bar{v}\|_{L^{4}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right).

(5.21)

Inserting (
5.3
) and (
5.3
) into (
5.3
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\nu\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{j}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right).

(5.22)

For any
s\in(t-1,t)
and
t\geq 1
, applying Gronwall’s lemma, then we can get that

\displaystyle\|A\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}-e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{s}^{t}Ce^{\int_{\eta}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ce^{\int_{t-1}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta.

Integrating w.r.t.
s
on
(t-1,t)
yields

\displaystyle\|A\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}-\int_{t-1}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ce^{\int_{t-1}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta.

(5.23)

Since our initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0}
and
\textbf{v}_{2,0}
belong to the
H^{1}
random absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}
, applying Gronwall’s lemma to (
5.10
), then we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(0)\|^{2},\quad t\geq 1.

Hence, we can get the following inequalities

\displaystyle\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{t-1}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(0)\|^{2},

and

\displaystyle\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}\int_{t-1}^{t}\!\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta.

Inserting these inequalities into (
5.3
), we also can deduce that

\displaystyle\|A\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}\int_{t-1}^{t}\!\left(1+\|A\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta

for any
t\geq 1
.
We replace
\omega
by
\theta_{-t}\omega
to get for any
t\geq 1

\displaystyle\|A\bar{v}(t,\theta_{-t}\omega,\bar{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{\int_{0}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}(\tau,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{i}(0))\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau-t}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{t-1}^{t}\!\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}(\eta,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta-t}\omega)|^{4}+1\right){\rm{d}}\eta.

(5.24)

By (
5.17
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[t-1,t]}\|A\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\leq 2\rho(\omega)+2\|\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2},\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)+1.

As a consequence, let

\displaystyle T_{\omega}:=T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)+1,\quad\omega\in\Omega,

we can get that

\displaystyle\int_{T_{\omega}-1}^{T_{\omega}}\left(\|A\textbf{v}_{1}(\eta,\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta-T_{\omega}}\omega)|^{4}\right){\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq 4\left(\rho(\omega)+\|\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right)^{2}+\int_{-1}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{4}\,{\rm{d}}\eta=:\zeta_{8}(\omega).

(5.25)

On the other hand, by (
3.4
), it yields

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}\leq C\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right)\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{6}\right).

we apply Gronwall’s lemma to deduce

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(t,\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int^{t}_{0}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(0)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}Ce^{\int^{t}_{s}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int^{t}_{0}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{2})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\left[\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(0)\|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}C\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}s\right].

Since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}C\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}s\leq e^{\int^{t}_{0}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{6})\,{\rm{d}}\tau},

then the estimate is given by

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(t,\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\leq\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(0)\|^{2}+1\right)e^{\int^{t}_{0}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{6})\,{\rm{d}}\tau},\quad t>0.

Hence, for
\textbf{v}(0)\in\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-t}\omega)
we can deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\eta,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{4}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-t}\omega)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+1\right)^{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\int^{\eta}_{0}C(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau-t}\omega)|^{6})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\leq\big(\zeta_{2}(\theta_{-t}\omega)+C\big)^{2}e^{Ct+C\int^{0}_{-t}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{6}\,{\rm{d}}\tau},\quad t>0,

and, particularly for
t=T_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T_{\omega}}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\eta,\theta_{-{T_{\omega}}}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{4}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big(\zeta_{2}(\theta_{-{T_{\omega}}}\omega)+C\big)^{2}e^{C{T_{\omega}}+C\int^{0}_{-{T_{\omega}}}|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{6}\,{\rm{d}}\tau}=:\zeta_{9}(\omega).

(5.26)

Finally, inserting (
5.25
) and (
5.3
) into (
5.3
) we can get at
t=T_{\omega}
that

\displaystyle\|A\bar{v}(T_{\omega},\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega,\bar{v}(0))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\zeta_{9}(\omega)}\zeta_{8}(\omega)\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}.

Hence, let

\displaystyle L_{4}(\mathfrak{B},\omega):=Ce^{C\zeta_{9}(\omega)}\zeta_{8}(\omega),\quad\omega\in\Omega.\qed

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.5
.

In this section, we next are ready to introduce the following main result.

Theorem 5.6

(
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing)
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. For any tempered set
\mathfrak{D}\in\mathcal{D}_{H}
, there
are random variables
T_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\cdot)
and
L_{\mathfrak{D}}(\cdot)
such that any two solutions
\textbf{v}_{1}
and
\textbf{v}_{2}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}
in
\mathfrak{D}\left(\theta_{-T_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega\right)
, respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\big\|\textbf{v}_{1}\!\big(T_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-T_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0}\big)-\textbf{v}_{2}\big(T_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega),\theta_{-T_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)}\omega,\textbf{v}_{2,0}\big)\big\|{{}^{2}_{H^{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega)\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}-\textbf{v}_{2,0}\|^{2},\quad\omega\in\Omega.

(5.27)

Proof.

By virtue of Lemma
5.3
, for any the tempered set
\mathfrak{D}\in\mathcal{D}_{H}
there are random variables
T_{1}(\mathfrak{D},\cdot)
and
L_{3}(\mathfrak{D},\cdot)
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{v}\left(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega,\bar{v}(0)\right)\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq L_{3}({\mathfrak{D}},\omega)\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2},

(5.28)

where
T_{1}=T_{1}(\mathfrak{D},\omega)
. Moreover,
\textbf{v}\left(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0}\right)\in\mathfrak{B}(\omega)
for any
\textbf{v}_{0}\in\mathfrak{D}(\theta_{-T_{1}}\omega)
,
\omega\in\Omega
.
Hence, by Lemma
5.5
and (
5.28
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{v}\big(T_{\omega}+T_{1},\theta_{-T_{\omega}-T_{1}}\omega,\bar{v}(0)\big)\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bar{v}\big(T_{\omega},\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega,\bar{v}(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{\omega}-T_{1}}\omega,\bar{v}(0))\big)\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq L_{4}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)\left\|\bar{v}\big(T_{1},\theta_{-T_{\omega}-T_{1}}\omega,\bar{v}(0)\big)\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\ \text{(by Lemma \ref{lemma5.1})}

\displaystyle\leq L_{4}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)L_{3}\big(\mathfrak{D},\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega\big)\|\bar{v}(0)\|^{2}\ \text{(by \eqref{mar19.6})},

where
T_{1}=T_{1}(\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega)
,
uniformly for any
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}\in\mathfrak{B}(\theta_{-T_{\omega}-T_{1}(\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega)}\omega)
,
\omega\in\Omega
. Thence,
let the random variables is given by

\displaystyle T_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega):=T_{\omega}+T_{1}(\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega),

\displaystyle L_{\mathfrak{D}}(\omega):=L_{4}(\mathfrak{B},\omega)L_{3}\big(\mathfrak{D},\theta_{-T_{\omega}}\omega\big).

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.6
.
∎

6
The
(H,H^{2})
-random attractor

In this section, we will prove that the random attractor
\mathcal{A}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise is in fact a finite-dimensional
(H,H^{2})
-random attractor. More precisely, Theorem
3.12
is now strengthened to as stated below.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let Assumption
1
hold and
f\in H
. Then the RDS
\phi
generated by the the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise has a tempered
(H,H^{2})
-random attractor
\mathcal{A}
. In addition,
\mathcal{A}
has finite fractal dimension in
H^{2}
: there exists a constant
d>0
such that

d_{f}^{H^{2}}\!\big(\mathcal{A}(\omega)\big)\leq d,\quad\omega\in\Omega.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.4
, we have also constructed an
H^{2}
random absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}_{H^{2}}
, which is tempered and closed in
H^{2}
. By the
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing property (
7.32
), we have proved the
(H,H^{2})
-asymptotic compactness of
\phi
. Hence, by Lemma
2.5
we can show that
\mathcal{A}
is indeed an
(H,H^{2})
-random attractor of
\phi
.
Since the fractal dimension in
H
of
\mathcal{A}
is finite (see the Theorem
3.12
), by the
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing property (
7.32
) and Lemma 5 in
[
21
]
, the finite-dimensionality in
H^{2}
is proved. This completes the proof of Theorem
6.1
.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

The
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing property (
7.32
) and the finite fractal dimension in
H^{2}
of the global attractor are new even for deterministic anisotropic NS equations.

7
The
(H,H^{3})
-random attractor

In this section, we first prove an
H^{3}
random absorbing set of system (
3.17
) as
f\in H^{2}
. Nextly, we will prove the (
H,H^{3}
)-smoothing effect of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise. This effect is essentially a local
(H,H^{3})
-Lipschitz continuity in initial values. Finally, we will prove that the random attractor
\mathcal{A}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise is in fact a finite-dimensional
(H,H^{3})
-random attractor.

Lemma 7.1
.

[
H^{2}
bound]
Let
f\in H^{1}
and Assumption
2
hold.
Then there exists a random variable
T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)>T_{1}(\omega)
such that, for any
t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)
,

\displaystyle\big\|A\textbf{v}(t,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\big\|^{2}+\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{2}\ {\rm{d}}s\leq\zeta_{10}(\omega),

where
\zeta_{10}(\omega)
is a tempered random variable given by (
7
) such that
\zeta_{10}(\omega)>\zeta_{1}(\omega)\geq 1
,
\omega\in\Omega
.

Proof.

Taking the inner product of the first equation (
3.17
) with
A^{2}\textbf{v}
in
H
, by integration by parts yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\Delta v_{1}\Delta v_{1}dx-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\Delta v_{2}\Delta v_{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\big(B(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)),A^{2}\textbf{v}\big)+(f,A^{2}\textbf{v})

\displaystyle+(-\nu A_{1}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega),A^{2}\textbf{v})+(hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega),A^{2}\textbf{v})

\displaystyle=:K_{1}(t)+K_{2}(t)+K_{3}(t)+K_{4}(t).

(7.1)

By direct computations and
\nabla\cdot\textbf{v}=0
and (
3.16
), it also yields

\displaystyle-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\Delta v_{1}\Delta v_{1}dx-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\Delta v_{2}\Delta v_{2}dx

\displaystyle=\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{y}\Delta v_{1})^{2}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{x}\Delta v_{2})^{2}dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}.

(7.2)

For the term
K_{1}(t)
, by integration by parts, by using the Hölder inequality, and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality and the Young inequality, we can get that for any
h\in H^{3}

\displaystyle K_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|_{L^{4}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|

\displaystyle+C\|\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|_{L^{4}}\|A(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|_{L^{4}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|\|A(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|

\displaystyle+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|\|A(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{\frac{1}{2}})\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|^{2}\|A(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|^{4}\|A(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|^{2}\|A(\textbf{v}+hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{4})\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{4})(\|Ahy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}hy_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)\|^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4})\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4})|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4})\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{6}).

(7.3)

For the last three terms
K_{2}(t)
-
K_{4}(t)
, by integration by parts, by using the Hölder inequality and the Young inequality, we can get that for
h\in H^{3}
and
f\in H^{1}

\displaystyle K_{2}(t)+K_{3}(t)+K_{4}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|+\nu\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}h\|^{2}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}h\|^{2}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}).

(7.4)

Inserting (
7
)-(
7
) into (
7
), it yields

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4})\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{6})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{4})\|A\textbf{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{t}\omega)|^{6}).

(7.5)

For
\eta\in(t-\frac{1}{2},t-\frac{1}{4})
, applying Gronwall’s lemma to (
7
) on
(\eta,t)
, we can get that

\displaystyle\|A\textbf{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{\eta}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}(s)\|^{2}{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{C\int_{\eta}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta)\|^{2}

\displaystyle+C\int_{\eta}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s}\omega)|^{6}){\rm{d}}s,

and integrating over
\eta\in(t-\frac{1}{2},t-\frac{1}{4})
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|A\textbf{v}(t)\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{8}\int_{t-\frac{1}{4}}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}(s)\|^{2}{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t-\frac{1}{4}}e^{C\int_{\eta}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta)\|^{2}{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle+C\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s)\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s}\omega)|^{6}){\rm{d}}s.

(7.6)

We replace
\omega
with
\vartheta_{-t}\omega
to deduce

\displaystyle\|A\textbf{v}(t,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{t-\frac{1}{4}}^{t}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\|A\textbf{v}(t,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{t-\frac{1}{4}}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\tau,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau+C\int_{s-t}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t-\frac{1}{4}}e^{C\int_{\eta}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\tau,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau+C\int_{\eta}^{t}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau-t}\omega)|^{4}{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle+C\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\tau,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau+C\int_{s}^{t}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau-t}\omega)|^{4}{\rm{d}}\tau}

\displaystyle(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s-t}\omega)|^{6}){\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\tau,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau+C\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}{\rm{d}}\tau}(\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t-\frac{1}{4}}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{6}+|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s-t}\omega)|^{6}){\rm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\tau,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}){\rm{d}}\tau+C\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4}{\rm{d}}\tau}(\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{6}){\rm{d}}s+\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s-t}\omega)|^{6}{\rm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{C\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}(1+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\tau,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{6}){\rm{d}}\tau+C\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{6}{\rm{d}}\tau}(\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{6}{\rm{d}}s+\int_{-1}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s}\omega)|^{6}{\rm{d}}s+1),

(7.7)

By (
4.12
), we also can deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}\|A\textbf{v}(\eta,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{3}(\omega),

(7.8)

\displaystyle\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}(\eta,\theta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}(0))\|^{6}\,{\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\leq|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{3},\quad t\geq T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega),

(7.9)

here
\zeta_{3}
is a tempered random variable defined in (
4.12
) and a random variable
T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega)
defined in (
4.11
), respectively.

Inserting (
7.8
) and (
7.9
) into (
7
) yields

\displaystyle\|A\textbf{v}(t,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{t-\frac{1}{4}}^{t}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{C(1+|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{3})+C\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{\tau}\omega)|^{6}{\rm{d}}\tau}\left(C|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{3}+C\int_{-1}^{0}|y_{\delta}(\vartheta_{s}\omega)|^{6}{\rm{d}}s+C\right)

\displaystyle:=\zeta_{10}(\omega).

(7.10)

This completes the proof of the Lemma

.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

[

\displaystyle\qquad+|\zeta_{3}(\omega)|^{3}+C\int^{0}_{-4}\left(1+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{s}\omega)|^{6}\right){\rm{d}}s,\quad t>T_{\mathfrak{B}}(\omega).

and
\eta\in[t-1,t]

(

, it can obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{t-\frac{1}{2}}^{t}\|A\textbf{v}(s,\vartheta_{-t}\omega,\textbf{v}_{0})\|^{6}{\rm{d}}s

7.1
(H,H^{3})
-smoothing

Let
k=2
or
k=3
. We introduce a random set given by

\mathfrak{B}^{k}=\{\mathfrak{B}^{k}(\omega)\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
in
H^{k}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}^{k}(\omega):=\left\{v\in H^{k}:\|A^{\frac{k}{2}}v\|^{2}\leq\zeta_{12}(\omega)\right\},\quad\omega\in\Omega,

(7.22)

where
\zeta_{12}(\cdot)
is the tempered random variable given by (
7
). In order to prove the
(H,H^{3})
-Lipschtiz, we need to prove the
(H,H^{2})
-Lipschtiz and
(H^{2},H^{3})
-Lipschtiz. By the similar method in Theorem
5.6
and
f\in H^{2}
, we will obtain the
(H,H^{2})
-smoothing. Moreover, we only prove the
(H^{2},H^{3})
-Lipschtiz in this subsection.

Lemma 7.3

(
(H^{2},H^{3})
-smoothing on
\mathfrak{B}^{2}
)
.

Let Assumption
2
hold and
f\in H^{2}
. There exist random variables
T_{\omega}^{*}
and
L_{5}(\mathfrak{B}^{2},\omega)
such that any two solutions
\textbf{v}_{1}
and
\textbf{v}_{2}
of the random anisotropic NS equations (
3.17
) driven by colored noise corresponding to initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0},

\textbf{v}_{2,0}
in
\mathfrak{B}^{2}(\theta_{-T_{\omega}^{*}}\omega)
, respectively, satisfy

\displaystyle\big\|\textbf{v}_{1}(T_{\omega}^{*},\theta_{-T_{\omega}^{*}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{1,0})-\textbf{v}_{2}(T_{\omega}^{*},\theta_{-T_{\omega}^{*}}\omega,\textbf{v}_{2,0})\big\|{{}^{2}_{H^{3}}}\leq L_{5}(\mathfrak{B}^{2},\omega)\|\textbf{v}_{1,0}-\textbf{v}_{2,0}\|^{2}_{H^{2}},\quad\omega\in\Omega.

Proof.

Taking the inner product of the system (
5.1
) with
A^{3}\bar{v}
in
H
, by integration by parts, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}\Delta^{2}\bar{v}_{1}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}\Delta^{2}\bar{v}_{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),\,A^{3}\bar{v}\big)-\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),\,A^{3}\bar{v}\big).

(7.23)

By direct computations and
\nabla\cdot\bar{v}=0
and (
3.16
), we also have

\displaystyle\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}\Delta^{2}\bar{v}_{1}dx-\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}\Delta^{2}\bar{v}_{2}dx

\displaystyle=\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1})^{2}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}\partial_{xy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1}dx

\displaystyle+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{2})^{2}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\partial_{xy}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}\partial_{xy}\Delta\bar{v}_{2}dx

\displaystyle=\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{1})^{2}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{yy}\Delta\bar{v}_{2})^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{2})^{2}dx+\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\partial_{xx}\Delta\bar{v}_{1})^{2}dx

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\|A^{2}\textbf{v}\|^{2}.

(7.24)

By using the Hölder inequality, the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality and the Young inequality, we can get that

\displaystyle\big|\big(B(\bar{v},\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)),\,A^{3}\bar{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A\bar{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\bar{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}(\textbf{v}_{1}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|+\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|A\bar{v}\|\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|\big(\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|+\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{1}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right),

(7.25)

and

\displaystyle\big|\big(B(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega),\bar{v}),\,A^{3}\bar{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\bar{v}\|_{L^{4}}\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)\|_{L^{4}}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|_{L^{4}}\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|A\bar{v}\|\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|\left(\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|+\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}h\||y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(\textbf{v}_{2}+hy_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega))\|\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu}{8}\|A^{2}\bar{v}\|^{2}+C\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{2}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right).

(7.26)

Inserting (
7.1
), (
7.1
) and (
7.1
) into (
7.1
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm{d}}}{{\rm{d}}t}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{j}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+C\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{j}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}\right).

For any
s\in(t-1,t)
and
t\geq 1
, applying Gronwall’s lemma, then we also can get that

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}-e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{s}^{t}Ce^{\int_{\eta}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{2}\right){\rm{d}}\eta

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ce^{\int_{t-1}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{2}\right){\rm{d}}\eta.

Integrating w.r.t.
s
on
(t-1,t)
yields

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}-\int_{t-1}^{t}e^{\int_{s}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\bar{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq Ce^{\int_{t-1}^{t}C(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{4}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\tau}\omega)|^{4})\,{\rm{d}}\tau}\int_{t-1}^{t}\|A\bar{v}\|^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|A^{\frac{3}{2}}\textbf{v}_{i}\|^{2}+|y_{\delta}(\theta_{\eta}\omega)|^{2}\right){\rm{d}}\eta.

(7.27)

Since our initial values
\textbf{v}_{1,0}
and

belong to the

random absorbing set
\mathfrak{B}^{2}
, applying Gronwall’s lemma to (
